## `reviews/` â€” convenÃ§Ã£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃ§Ã£o

EvidÃªncia de rodada, mesmo papel que `docs/architecture/history/` tem para decisÃµes de arquitetura: nunca normativo, sÃ³ prova de como uma nota/decisÃ£o foi alcanÃ§ada. TrÃªs geraÃ§Ãµes de conteÃºdo coexistem aqui, nesta ordem cronolÃ³gica:

2. **ConvergÃªncia dos critÃ©rios por eixo** (`joint-review-criteria-round1-*`, `security-axis-criteria-round1-*`, `remaining-axes-round1-*`, `audit-areas-*`) â€” produziram `joint-review-criteria.md`.

   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-prompt.txt` / `-codex-prompt-round2.txt`, ... â€” prompt enviado ao Codex CLI, um por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-output-round1.txt`, `-round2.txt`, ... â€” saÃ­da bruta do Codex CLI. Alguns trechos que ecoam saÃ­da de outros comandos de shell continham mojibake (encoding double/triple-decoded em algumas invocaÃ§Ãµes via PowerShell) â€” revertido deterministicamente para os arquivos anteriores a cada rodada (achado real de higiene, corrigido, nÃ£o sÃ³ mitigado); a saÃ­da de uma rodada em andamento pode ainda conter mojibake atÃ© a rodada seguinte revisÃ¡-la, por ser evidÃªncia datada do que o Codex realmente viu naquele momento â€” nunca editada retroativamente. Se precisar do conteÃºdo, prefira o `-summary.md` correspondente, que jÃ¡ extrai a informaÃ§Ã£o sem o ruÃ­do de encoding.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` â€” **o Ãºnico arquivo desta trinca que deveria ser lido diretamente.** Nota final por critÃ©rio (ambos os lados), achados corrigidos com commit real, achados restantes classificados em "impedimento externo real" vs. "escopo maior que correÃ§Ã£o pontual".

Arquivos de transcript bruto (`.txt`, prefixo `_`, ou os `full-audit-*-codex-output-*.txt`) nunca sÃ£o fonte de verdade por si sÃ³ â€” sempre prefira o `.md` de resumo/nota que os acompanha. **PolÃ­tica de proveniÃªncia/metadata**: os `.md` de nota/resumo carregam frontmatter (`status`/`owner`/`authority`, mais `Last verified` nos routers); os `.txt` brutos nÃ£o â€” exigir frontmatter em cada transcript violaria proporcionalidade (`principles.md` #1) sem ganho real, jÃ¡ que sua proveniÃªncia Ã© herdada do `.md` que os acompanha (mesmo prefixo de nome) e da posiÃ§Ã£o cronolÃ³gica descrita nesta seÃ§Ã£o. Isso Ã© uma decisÃ£o de design, nÃ£o uma lacuna: um transcript nunca Ã© lido isoladamente por convenÃ§Ã£o (ver acima), entÃ£o nÃ£o precisa carregar sua prÃ³pria metadata.

# CritÃ©rios de revisÃ£o conjunta Claudeâ†”Codex, por eixo

53:const DIACRITICS_PATTERN = new RegExp(`[\\u0300-\\u036f]`, "g");

60:    .trim()
61:    .toLowerCase()
62:    .replace(/\s+/g, " ");

1:/**
2: * Authorization matrix — implementation-blueprint.md §4.3. "A matriz é código
3: * versionado e testado, não documentação informal."
4: */
5:import type { RequestContext } from "./request-context.js";
6:
7:export type Action =
8:  | "item:create"
9:  | "item:read"
10:  | "item:update"
11:  | "item:delete"
12:  | "reminder:manage"
13:  | "document:reserve-upload"
14:  | "document:read"
15:  | "document:delete"
16:  | "extraction:confirm"
17:  | "notification:configure"
18:  | "audit:read"
19:  | "system:ping"; // M1 test route action, not in the blueprint's business list but
20:// declared explicitly here rather than silently bypassing the matrix (see report).
21:
22:export interface AuthorizedResource {
23:  tenantId: string;
24:  ownerUserId?: string;
25:  assigneeUserId?: string;
26:  status?: string;
27:}
28:
29:export interface AuthorizationInput {
30:  context: RequestContext;
31:  action: Action;
32:  resource?: AuthorizedResource;
33:}
34:
35:/**
36: * Minimal role model for M1 (MVP: tenantId=userId, single-owner tenants). Membership/roles
37: * beyond OWNER are FUT-001 (Organization/Membership) per data-model.md — the matrix is
38: * structured so adding roles later doesn't change call sites, only this table.
39: */
40:export type Role = "OWNER" | "MEMBER" | "VIEWER";
41:
42:const READ_ONLY_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER", "MEMBER", "VIEWER"]);
43:const WRITE_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER", "MEMBER"]);
44:const ADMIN_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER"]);
45:
46:const ACTION_ROLES: Record<Action, ReadonlySet<Role>> = {
47:  "item:create": WRITE_ROLES,
48:  "item:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
49:  "item:update": WRITE_ROLES,
50:  "item:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
51:  "reminder:manage": WRITE_ROLES,
52:  "document:reserve-upload": WRITE_ROLES,
53:  "document:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
54:  "document:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
55:  "extraction:confirm": WRITE_ROLES,
56:  "notification:configure": ADMIN_ROLES,
57:  "audit:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
58:  "system:ping": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
59:};
60:
61:export class AuthorizationDeniedError extends Error {
62:  constructor(
63:    readonly reason:
64:      | "TENANT_MISMATCH"
65:      | "NO_MEMBERSHIP"
66:      | "INSUFFICIENT_ROLE"
67:      | "RESOURCE_OWNERSHIP_MISMATCH",
68:    action: Action,
69:  ) {
70:    super(`Authorization denied for action "${action}": ${reason}`);
71:    this.name = "AuthorizationDeniedError";
72:  }
73:}
74:
75:/**
76: * authorize() — implementation-blueprint.md §4.3: "authorize() verifica primeiro
77: * igualdade de tenant, depois papel e vínculo por recurso." Throws
78: * AuthorizationDeniedError (never returns false) so callers cannot forget to check
79: * a boolean result — the http layer maps this to AuthorizationError (403).
80: *
81: * Order matters and is deliberately fail-closed at every step:
82: *  1. context must carry at least one role (no membership => no access at all).
83: *  2. if a resource is supplied, resource.tenantId MUST equal context.tenant.tenantId —
84: *     this is the check that makes cross-tenant ID substitution fail, independent of
85: *     role, and independent of what the caller *claims* the resource's tenant is (the
86: *     resource object here must always be populated from a DB read keyed by the
87: *     authenticated tenantId, never from client input — see repository contract).
88: *  3. role must be sufficient for the action.
89: *  4. if the resource declares an owner/assignee, membership role alone isn't enough
90: *     for actions that are resource-scoped by ownership in the future; MVP has no
91: *     per-item ownership finer than tenant, so this step is a no-op today but kept
92: *     explicit so a future item-level ACL doesn't require re-deriving this function.
93: */
94:export function authorize(input: AuthorizationInput): void {
95:  const { context, action, resource } = input;
96:  const roles = context.tenant.roles as Role[];
97:
98:  if (roles.length === 0) {
99:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("NO_MEMBERSHIP", action);
100:  }
101:
102:  if (resource && resource.tenantId !== context.tenant.tenantId) {
103:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("TENANT_MISMATCH", action);
104:  }
105:
106:  const allowedRoles = ACTION_ROLES[action];
107:  const hasRole = roles.some((role) => allowedRoles.has(role));
108:  if (!hasRole) {
109:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("INSUFFICIENT_ROLE", action);
110:  }
111:
112:  if (resource?.assigneeUserId && resource.ownerUserId) {
113:    const isOwnerOrAssignee =
114:      resource.ownerUserId === context.principal.userId ||
115:      resource.assigneeUserId === context.principal.userId;
116:    // OWNER role bypasses per-resource ownership (tenant-wide admin), MEMBER/VIEWER
117:    // scoped resources would require ownership match once per-item ACLs exist. MVP:
118:    // tenant-wide access for any authenticated member is the approved model (single
119:    // owner per tenant today), so this is enforced only when both fields ARE present
120:    // (defensive for future use), never invented from nothing.
121:    if (!roles.includes("OWNER") && !isOwnerOrAssignee) {
122:      throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("RESOURCE_OWNERSHIP_MISMATCH", action);
123:    }
124:  }
125:}

54:      indexName: "GSI1",

71:      sortKey: { name: "GSI3SK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },

73:    });

76:    this.table.addGlobalSecondaryIndex({
77:      indexName: "GSI4",
78:      partitionKey: { name: "GSI4PK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },
79:      sortKey: { name: "GSI4SK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },
80:    });

86:      sortKey: { name: "GSI5SK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },
87:    });
88:

90:    this.table.addGlobalSecondaryIndex({
91:      indexName: "GSI6",
92:      partitionKey: { name: "GSI6PK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },
93:      sortKey: { name: "GSI6SK", type: dynamodb.AttributeType.STRING },
94:    });
95:  }
96:
97:  /**
98:   * Resource ARNs for every GSI EXCEPT GSI3 (data-model.md §3's isolation safeguard).
99:   *
100:   * M3 correction: CDK's built-in `Table.grantReadWriteData`/`grantReadData`/`grant()`
101:   * helpers always include a blanket `<tableArn>/index/*` resource whenever the table has
102:   * ANY index - there is no way to exclude a single GSI through them. Using those helpers
103:   * for tenant-facing functions would silently grant `dynamodb:Query` on GSI3 to every
104:   * such function, contradicting the documented safeguard ("IAM do índice restrito
105:   * exclusivamente ao ReminderProducer"). This construct therefore builds its own
106:   * PolicyStatements with an explicit resource list (base table + the 5 non-GSI3 indexes)
107:   * instead of delegating to those helpers.
108:   */
109:  private tenantFacingResources(): string[] {
110:    const indexNames = ["GSI1", "GSI2", "GSI4", "GSI5"];
111:    return [
112:      this.table.tableArn,
113:      ...indexNames.map((name) => `${this.table.tableArn}/index/${name}`),
114:    ];
115:  }
116:
117:  private gsi3Resource(): string {
118:    return `${this.table.tableArn}/index/GSI3`;
119:  }
120:
121:  private gsi6Resource(): string {
122:    return `${this.table.tableArn}/index/GSI6`;
123:  }
124:
125:  /** General read/write grant on the base table + GSI1/GSI2/GSI4/GSI5 (tenant-scoped indexes). Never includes GSI3/GSI6 - see tenantFacingResources() above. */
126:  grantReadWriteData(grantee: IGrantable) {
127:    grantee.grantPrincipal.addToPrincipalPolicy(
128:      new iam.PolicyStatement({
129:        actions: [
130:          "dynamodb:BatchGetItem",
131:          "dynamodb:GetItem",
132:          "dynamodb:Query",
133:          "dynamodb:Scan",
134:          "dynamodb:BatchWriteItem",
135:          "dynamodb:PutItem",
136:          "dynamodb:UpdateItem",
137:          "dynamodb:DeleteItem",
138:          "dynamodb:ConditionCheckItem",
139:          "dynamodb:DescribeTable",
140:        ],
141:        resources: this.tenantFacingResources(),
142:      }),
143:    );
144:    if (this.table.encryptionKey) {
145:      this.table.encryptionKey.grantEncryptDecrypt(grantee);
146:    }
147:  }
148:
149:  grantReadData(grantee: IGrantable) {
150:    grantee.grantPrincipal.addToPrincipalPolicy(
151:      new iam.PolicyStatement({
152:        actions: ["dynamodb:BatchGetItem", "dynamodb:GetItem", "dynamodb:Query", "dynamodb:Scan", "dynamodb:DescribeTable"],
153:        resources: this.tenantFacingResources(),
154:      }),
155:    );
156:    if (this.table.encryptionKey) {
157:      this.table.encryptionKey.grantDecrypt(grantee);
158:    }
159:  }
160:
161:  /** The ONLY sanctioned way to read GSI3 - resource is the GSI3 index ARN exclusively (never the table or any other index), intended solely for the M3 ReminderProducer. test/infra/stack.test.ts's isolation test asserts no other function's synthesized IAM policy references this index. */
162:  grantGsi3ReadTo(grantee: IGrantable) {
163:    grantee.grantPrincipal.addToPrincipalPolicy(
164:      new iam.PolicyStatement({
165:        actions: ["dynamodb:Query", "dynamodb:GetItem"],
166:        resources: [this.gsi3Resource()],
167:      }),
168:    );
169:    if (this.table.encryptionKey) {
170:      this.table.encryptionKey.grantDecrypt(grantee);
171:    }
172:  }
173:
174:  /** The ONLY sanctioned way to read GSI6 - resource is the GSI6 index ARN exclusively.
175:   * `docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md` fixes exactly two callers:
176:   * `ReminderReconciliation` (WORKSTATE#CLAIMED/WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING candidates) and
177:   * `OutboxSweeperReminderDispatch` (RECON#OUTBOX#PENDING). test/infra/stack.test.ts's
178:   * isolation test asserts no other function's synthesized IAM policy references this index. */
179:  grantGsi6ReadTo(grantee: IGrantable) {
180:    grantee.grantPrincipal.addToPrincipalPolicy(
181:      new iam.PolicyStatement({
182:        actions: ["dynamodb:Query", "dynamodb:GetItem"],
183:        resources: [this.gsi6Resource()],
184:      }),
185:    );
186:    if (this.table.encryptionKey) {
187:      this.table.encryptionKey.grantDecrypt(grantee);
188:    }
189:  }

1:/**
2: * IdentityMapping repository — data-model.md §2/§"IdentityMapping" +
3: * implementation-blueprint.md §23.1/§7.3: PK=IDENTITY#cognitoSub#<sub>, SK=MAP, created
4: * atomically (ConditionExpression attribute_not_exists(PK)) on first login. This is one
5: * of the two documented exceptions to the "every key starts with TENANT#tenantId" rule
6: * (the other is GSI3) — the lookup happens *before* tenantId is known.
7: */
8:import type { EntityKey, IdentityStore } from "../ports/identity-store.js";
9:import { InternalError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
10:
11:export interface IdentityMapping {
12:  PK: string;
13:  SK: "MAP";
14:  entityType: "IdentityMapping";
15:  cognitoSub: string;
16:  userId: string;
17:  tenantId: string;
18:  createdAt: string;
19:}
20:
21:export function identityMappingKey(cognitoSub: string): EntityKey {
22:  return { PK: `IDENTITY#cognitoSub#${cognitoSub}`, SK: "MAP" };
23:}
24:
25:export class IdentityMappingRepository {
26:  constructor(
27:    private readonly store: IdentityStore,
28:    private readonly now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
29:  ) {}
30:
31:  async find(cognitoSub: string): Promise<IdentityMapping | undefined> {
32:    return this.store.get<IdentityMapping>(identityMappingKey(cognitoSub));
33:  }
34:
35:  /**
36:   * Atomically creates the mapping on first login. MVP: tenantId=userId (data-model.md
37:   * §7.3 "No MVP, tenantId=userId, mas o código recebe ambos como conceitos separados"),
38:   * so newUserId is used for both — callers must never derive one from the other outside
39:   * this function once Organizations (FUT-001) exist.
40:   *
41:   * Idempotent under races: if two concurrent first-logins for the same sub both call
42:   * this, exactly one PutItem wins (attribute_not_exists(PK)); the loser re-reads and
43:   * returns the winner's mapping instead of erroring, so first-login is safe to retry.
44:   */
45:  async findOrCreate(cognitoSub: string, newUserId: string, newTenantId: string): Promise<IdentityMapping> {
46:    const existing = await this.find(cognitoSub);
47:    if (existing) {
48:      return existing;
49:    }
50:
51:    const key = identityMappingKey(cognitoSub);
52:    const candidate: IdentityMapping = {
53:      ...key,
54:      SK: "MAP",
55:      entityType: "IdentityMapping",
56:      cognitoSub,
57:      userId: newUserId,
58:      tenantId: newTenantId,
59:      createdAt: this.now(),
60:    };
61:
62:    const created = await this.store.putIfAbsent(candidate);
63:    if (created) {
64:      return candidate;
65:    }
66:
67:    // Lost the race: someone else created it first-login-atomically between our
68:    // find() and putIfAbsent(). Re-read; if it's still missing something is badly
69:    // wrong (a delete of an IdentityMapping is not a supported operation).
70:    const winner = await this.find(cognitoSub);
71:    if (!winner) {
72:      throw new InternalError("IdentityMapping vanished after losing creation race.", { cognitoSub });
73:    }
74:    return winner;
75:  }
76:}

4: * the shared validator both production code (validating inbound SQS/webhook payloads per

6: *
7: * Judgment call: ajv + ajv-formats chosen for JSON Schema validation - the blueprint names

9: * standard, actively maintained, zero-install-script.

20:import Ajv2020, { type ValidateFunction } from "ajv/dist/2020.js";
21:import addFormats from "ajv-formats";

23:// Statically imported schema modules for `defaultSchemaRegistry` (production runtime path).
24:import domainEventEnvelopeV1 from "../../../schemas/events/domain-event-envelope.v1.json";
25:import commandEnvelopeV1 from "../../../schemas/queues/command-envelope.v1.json";

34:export class SchemaRegistry {
35:  private readonly ajv: Ajv2020;
36:  private readonly compiled = new Map<string, ValidateFunction>();
37:
38:  /** Takes an explicit list of already-loaded schema objects - no filesystem access. Callers
39:   * that need "every schema under schemas/" (CLI/tests) use
40:   * `schema-registry-disk.ts#loadAllSchemasFromDisk()` instead of constructing this directly
41:   * with an empty/missing list. */
42:  constructor(schemas: object[]) {
43:    this.ajv = new Ajv2020({ strict: true, allErrors: true });
44:    addFormats(this.ajv);
45:    // Two passes not needed here since all schemas are added before any compile() call below
46:    // (compile is lazy, triggered by the first validate() per schemaId) - $ref between
47:    // schemas resolves correctly regardless of list order.
48:    for (const schema of schemas) {
49:      const withId = schema as { $id?: string };
50:      if (withId.$id) {
51:        this.ajv.addSchema(schema, withId.$id);
52:      }
53:    }
54:  }
55:
56:  /** Validates `data` against the schema identified by its `$id`. Returns { valid, errors }. */
57:  validate(schemaId: string, data: unknown): { valid: boolean; errors: string[] } {
58:    let validateFn = this.compiled.get(schemaId);
59:    if (!validateFn) {
60:      const fn = this.ajv.getSchema(schemaId);
61:      if (!fn) {
62:        throw new Error(`Unknown schema $id: ${schemaId}`);
63:      }
64:      validateFn = fn;
65:      this.compiled.set(schemaId, validateFn);
66:    }
67:    const valid = validateFn(data) as boolean;
68:    const errors = (validateFn.errors ?? []).map(
69:      (e) => `${e.instancePath || "/"} ${e.message ?? "invalid"}`,
70:    );
71:    return { valid, errors };
72:  }
73:}
74:
75:/**
76: * Production runtime singleton - imported by Lambda handlers (e.g. reminder-dispatch-
77: * handler.ts). Built from statically-imported schema modules so esbuild inlines them as JS
78: * object literals into the bundle at build time, same fix as Redactor's
79: * schemas/sensitive-fields.json (Arquitetura axis round1, commit 494f4e5). Do NOT change
80: * this back to `new SchemaRegistry()` (dynamic directory walk) - that depends on
81: * `import.meta.url`, which is empty under the esbuild "cjs" bundle format
82: * (infra/lib/scoped-lambda-function.ts's bundleEntry) and would silently resolve zero
83: * schemas at real Lambda cold start. New schema added under schemas/ that a handler needs
84: * at runtime: add both the file AND a static import line above.
85: */

87:  domainEventEnvelopeV1,

4: * is NOT a blocker for M1 per that same doc - implemented here as a configurable prop
5: * (default OPTIONAL, never hardcoded to OFF/REQUIRED) so enabling REQUIRED later is a
6: * one-line prop change, not a rewrite.

8:import * as cognito from "aws-cdk-lib/aws-cognito";
9:import { Duration, RemovalPolicy } from "aws-cdk-lib";
10:import { Construct } from "constructs";
11:
12:export type MfaPolicy = "OFF" | "OPTIONAL" | "REQUIRED";
13:
14:export interface ExpirationTrackerAuthProps {
15:  /** UNK-006 still pending external research - default OPTIONAL, override once decided. */
16:  mfaPolicy?: MfaPolicy;
17:  removalPolicy?: RemovalPolicy;
18:}
19:
20:const MFA_MAP: Record<MfaPolicy, cognito.Mfa> = {
21:  OFF: cognito.Mfa.OFF,
22:  OPTIONAL: cognito.Mfa.OPTIONAL,
23:  REQUIRED: cognito.Mfa.REQUIRED,
24:};
25:
26:export class ExpirationTrackerAuth extends Construct {
27:  readonly userPool: cognito.UserPool;
28:  readonly userPoolClient: cognito.UserPoolClient;
29:
30:  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: ExpirationTrackerAuthProps = {}) {
31:    super(scope, id);
32:
33:    const mfaPolicy = props.mfaPolicy ?? "OPTIONAL";
34:
35:    this.userPool = new cognito.UserPool(this, "UserPool", {
36:      selfSignUpEnabled: true,
37:      signInAliases: { email: true },
38:      autoVerify: { email: true },
39:      standardAttributes: {
40:        email: { required: true, mutable: false },
41:      },
42:      passwordPolicy: {
43:        minLength: 12,
44:        requireLowercase: true,
45:        requireUppercase: true,
46:        requireDigits: true,
47:        requireSymbols: true,
48:      },
49:      mfa: MFA_MAP[mfaPolicy],
50:      mfaSecondFactor: { sms: false, otp: true }, // TOTP only - avoids SMS provider/cost dependency (COST/SEC judgment call, see report)
51:      accountRecovery: cognito.AccountRecovery.EMAIL_ONLY,
52:      removalPolicy: props.removalPolicy ?? RemovalPolicy.RETAIN,
53:      deviceTracking: {
54:        challengeRequiredOnNewDevice: true,
55:        deviceOnlyRememberedOnUserPrompt: true,
56:      },
57:    });
58:
59:    this.userPoolClient = this.userPool.addClient("WebClient", {
60:      authFlows: { userSrp: true },
61:      generateSecret: true, // BFF session pattern (§4.2): client secret held server-side only, never in the browser
62:      // Access token short-lived per blueprint §4.2 (5-15 min target); refresh rotation
63:      // + reuse detection is enforced by the BFF /session/refresh endpoint (M1's session
64:      // module), not by Cognito's own (non-rotating) refresh token alone.
65:      accessTokenValidity: Duration.minutes(15),
66:      idTokenValidity: Duration.minutes(15),
67:      refreshTokenValidity: Duration.days(30),
68:      preventUserExistenceErrors: true,
69:      oAuth: {
70:        flows: { authorizationCodeGrant: true },
71:        scopes: [cognito.OAuthScope.EMAIL, cognito.OAuthScope.OPENID, cognito.OAuthScope.PROFILE],
72:      },

114:    const ctxB = await resolver.resolve({ claims: claims("sub-B"), requestId: "r2", correlationId: "c2" });

116:
117:    expect(() => authorize({ context: ctxA, action: "document:read", resource: gsiResultFromTenantB })).toThrow(
118:      AuthorizationDeniedError,
119:    );
120:  });
121:
122:  it("end-to-end: tenant A's authenticated request never returns tenant B's tenantId, and each tenant has an independent quota bucket", async () => {
123:    const resA1 = await handleTestRoute({ resolver, quota }, { requestId: "r1", correlationId: "c1", claims: claims("sub-A") });
124:    const resB1 = await handleTestRoute({ resolver, quota }, { requestId: "r2", correlationId: "c2", claims: claims("sub-B") });
125:
126:    expect(resA1.statusCode).toBe(200);
127:    expect(resB1.statusCode).toBe(200);
128:    expect(resA1.body["tenantId"]).not.toBe(resB1.body["tenantId"]);
129:
130:    // Exhaust tenant A's route quota; tenant B is unaffected (independent bucket).
131:    for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
132:      await handleTestRoute({ resolver, quota }, { requestId: `ra-${i}`, correlationId: "c", claims: claims("sub-A") });
133:    }
134:    const exhaustedA = await handleTestRoute(
135:      { resolver, quota },
136:      { requestId: "ra-final", correlationId: "c", claims: claims("sub-A") },
137:    );
138:    expect(exhaustedA.statusCode).toBe(429);
139:
140:    const stillOkB = await handleTestRoute(
141:      { resolver, quota },
142:      { requestId: "rb-final", correlationId: "c", claims: claims("sub-B") },
143:    );
144:    expect(stillOkB.statusCode).toBe(200);
145:  });

148:    await resolver.resolve({ claims: claims("sub-A"), requestId: "r1", correlationId: "c1" });
149:    const ctxB = await resolver.resolve({ claims: claims("sub-B"), requestId: "r2", correlationId: "c2" });

1:/**
2: * HTTP handlers for the Expiration module — mirrors identity's
3: * http/test-route-handler.ts pipeline (resolve -> service, which internally calls
4: * authorize()) and its AppError -> status-code mapping, so every route in the system
5: * fails the same way.
6: */
7:import { AppError, ValidationError, toAppError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
8:import { AuthorizationDeniedError } from "../../identity/domain/authorization.js";
9:import { AuthorizationError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
10:import type { RequestContextResolver, ValidatedClaims } from "../../identity/application/resolve-request-context.js";
11:import type { ExpirationService } from "../application/expiration-service.js";
12:import type { CreateItemInput, RenewItemInput, UpdateItemInput } from "../domain/expiration-item.js";
13:
14:export interface HttpRequest<TBody = unknown> {
15:  requestId: string;
16:  correlationId: string;
17:  claims: ValidatedClaims;
18:  pathParameters?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
19:  queryStringParameters?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
20:  headers?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
21:  body?: TBody;
22:}
23:
24:export interface HttpResponse {
25:  statusCode: number;
26:  body: Record<string, unknown>;
27:}
28:
29:export interface ExpirationHttpDeps {
30:  resolver: RequestContextResolver;
31:  expiration: ExpirationService;
32:}
33:
34:const STATUS_BY_CATEGORY: Record<string, number> = {
35:  VALIDATION: 400,
36:  AUTH: 401,
37:  AUTHORIZATION: 403,
38:  NOT_FOUND: 404,
39:  CONFLICT: 409,
40:  QUOTA_EXCEEDED: 429,
41:  DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE: 503,
42:  INTERNAL: 500,
43:};
44:
45:function toResponse(appError: AppError): HttpResponse {
46:  const status = STATUS_BY_CATEGORY[appError.category] ?? 500;
47:  return { statusCode: status, body: appError.toJSON() };
48:}
49:
50:async function withErrorMapping(fn: () => Promise<HttpResponse>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
51:  try {
52:    return await fn();
53:  } catch (err) {
54:    const appError =
55:      err instanceof AuthorizationDeniedError
56:        ? new AuthorizationError(err.message, { reason: err.reason })
57:        : err instanceof AppError
58:          ? err
59:          : toAppError(err);
60:    return toResponse(appError);
61:  }
62:}
63:
64:function requireItemId(req: HttpRequest): string {
65:  const itemId = req.pathParameters?.["itemId"];
66:  if (!itemId) {
67:    throw new ValidationError("Missing itemId path parameter.");
68:  }
69:  return itemId;
70:}
71:
72:function requireExpectedVersion(req: HttpRequest): number {
73:  const raw = req.headers?.["if-match"] ?? req.queryStringParameters?.["expectedVersion"];
74:  const version = Number(raw);
75:  if (!raw || Number.isNaN(version)) {
76:    throw new ValidationError("Missing or invalid expected version (If-Match header).");
77:  }
78:  return version;
79:}
80:
81:export async function handleCreateItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest<CreateItemInput>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
82:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
83:    if (!req.body) throw new ValidationError("Missing request body.");
84:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
85:    const item = await deps.expiration.createItem(context, req.body);
86:    return { statusCode: 201, body: { item } };
87:  });
88:}
89:
90:export async function handleGetItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest): Promise<HttpResponse> {
91:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
92:    const itemId = requireItemId(req);
93:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
94:    const item = await deps.expiration.getItem(context, itemId);
95:    return { statusCode: 200, body: { item } };
96:  });
97:}
98:
99:export async function handleUpdateItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest<UpdateItemInput>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
100:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
101:    const itemId = requireItemId(req);
102:    if (!req.body) throw new ValidationError("Missing request body.");
103:    const expectedVersion = requireExpectedVersion(req);
104:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
105:    const item = await deps.expiration.updateItem(context, itemId, req.body, expectedVersion);
106:    return { statusCode: 200, body: { item } };
107:  });
108:}
109:
110:export async function handleArchiveItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest): Promise<HttpResponse> {
111:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
112:    const itemId = requireItemId(req);
113:    const expectedVersion = requireExpectedVersion(req);
114:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
115:    await deps.expiration.archiveItem(context, itemId, expectedVersion);
116:    return { statusCode: 204, body: {} };
117:  });
118:}
119:
120:export async function handleDeleteItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest): Promise<HttpResponse> {
121:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
122:    const itemId = requireItemId(req);
123:    const expectedVersion = requireExpectedVersion(req);
124:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
125:    await deps.expiration.deleteItem(context, itemId, expectedVersion);
126:    return { statusCode: 204, body: {} };
127:  });
128:}
129:
130:export async function handleRenewItem(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest<RenewItemInput>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
131:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
132:    const itemId = requireItemId(req);
133:    if (!req.body) throw new ValidationError("Missing request body.");
134:    const expectedVersion = requireExpectedVersion(req);
135:    const idempotencyKey = req.headers?.["idempotency-key"];
136:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
137:    const item = await deps.expiration.renewItem(context, itemId, req.body, expectedVersion, idempotencyKey);
138:    return { statusCode: 201, body: { item } };
139:  });
140:}
141:
142:export async function handleDashboard(deps: ExpirationHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest): Promise<HttpResponse> {
143:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
144:    const status = (req.queryStringParameters?.["status"] ?? "ACTIVE") as "ACTIVE" | "ARCHIVED" | "RENEWED" | "DELETED";
145:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId });
146:    const items = await deps.expiration.listDashboard(context, { status });
147:    return { statusCode: 200, body: { items } };
148:  });

4: * dispatch's own claim-by-occurrenceId already provides it, FIFO would add throughput
5: * limits without removing that need), maxReceiveCount=5 per implementation-blueprint.md

8:import { Duration } from "aws-cdk-lib";
9:import * as sqs from "aws-cdk-lib/aws-sqs";
10:import * as cloudwatch from "aws-cdk-lib/aws-cloudwatch";
11:import { Construct } from "constructs";
12:
13:export interface ReminderDispatchQueueProps {
14:  /** Must be >= 6x this value (visibility timeout sizing rule). Defaults to the
15:   * ReminderDispatch Lambda's own default timeout (10s, ScopedLambdaFunction). */
16:  consumerTimeoutSeconds?: number;
17:}
18:
19:export class ReminderDispatchQueue extends Construct {
20:  readonly queue: sqs.Queue;

24:    super(scope, id);
25:

27:      retentionPeriod: Duration.days(14),
28:      encryption: sqs.QueueEncryption.SQS_MANAGED,
29:    });
30:
31:    const consumerTimeout = props.consumerTimeoutSeconds ?? 10;
32:    this.queue = new sqs.Queue(this, "Queue", {
33:      retentionPeriod: Duration.days(4),
34:      visibilityTimeout: Duration.seconds(consumerTimeout * 6),
35:      receiveMessageWaitTime: Duration.seconds(20), // long polling
36:      encryption: sqs.QueueEncryption.SQS_MANAGED,
37:      deadLetterQueue: { queue: this.deadLetterQueue, maxReceiveCount: 5 },
38:    });

41:    new cloudwatch.Alarm(this, "DlqAgeAlarm", {
42:      metric: this.deadLetterQueue.metricApproximateAgeOfOldestMessage({ period: Duration.minutes(5) }),
43:      threshold: Duration.hours(1).toSeconds(),
44:      evaluationPeriods: 1,
45:      comparisonOperator: cloudwatch.ComparisonOperator.GREATER_THAN_THRESHOLD,
46:      alarmDescription: "ReminderDispatchQueue DLQ has messages older than 1 hour - investigate and redrive per runbook (never blind redrive).",
47:    });

 succeeded in 1458ms:
1:/**
2: * resolveRequestContext — implementation-blueprint.md §4.1/§7.2, the central resolver.
3: * Steps (verbatim from the blueprint):
4: *  1. validation already performed by the API Gateway authorizer (JWT signature/exp) —
5: *     this function receives already-validated claims, it does not verify JWT signatures;
6: *  2. consistent lookup of cognitoSub -> userId (IdentityMapping, atomic first-login create);
7: *  3. session/device resolution;
8: *  4. tenant/membership resolution;
9: *  5. reject tokens issued before globalLogoutAfter/deviceLogoutAfter;
10: *  6. build the immutable RequestContext.
11: */
12:import { AuthenticationError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
13:import { IdentityMappingRepository } from "../persistence/identity-mapping-repository.js";
14:import { UserRepository } from "../persistence/user-repository.js";
15:import type { RequestContext } from "../domain/request-context.js";
16:
17:/** Claims already validated (signature + expiry) by the API Gateway JWT authorizer. */
18:export interface ValidatedClaims {
19:  sub: string;
20:  tokenId: string; // jti
21:  issuedAt: string; // iat, ISO-8601
22:  expiresAt: string; // exp, ISO-8601
23:  deviceId?: string;
24:}
25:
26:export interface ResolveRequestContextInput {
27:  claims: ValidatedClaims;
28:  requestId: string;
29:  correlationId: string;
30:}
31:
32:export interface IdGenerator {
33:  newUserId(): string;
34:  newSessionId(): string;
35:}
36:
37:export class RequestContextResolver {
38:  constructor(
39:    private readonly identityMappings: IdentityMappingRepository,
40:    private readonly users: UserRepository,
41:    private readonly ids: IdGenerator,
42:  ) {}
43:
44:  async resolve(input: ResolveRequestContextInput): Promise<RequestContext> {
45:    const { claims } = input;
46:
47:    // Step 2: cognitoSub -> userId/tenantId. MVP tenantId=userId (data-model.md §7.3),
48:    // decided here and nowhere else so a future Organization model changes one call site.
49:    const newUserId = this.ids.newUserId();
50:    const mapping = await this.identityMappings.findOrCreate(claims.sub, newUserId, newUserId);
51:
52:    // Step 3/4: profile + membership. First-login also provisions the User profile
53:    // (OWNER role, MVP single-tenant-per-user) so resolveContext never returns a
54:    // context for a mapping with no backing profile.
55:    let profile = await this.users.getProfile(mapping.tenantId, mapping.userId);
56:    if (!profile) {
57:      profile = await this.users.createProfileIfAbsent({
58:        userId: mapping.userId,
59:        tenantId: mapping.tenantId,
60:        identitySubject: claims.sub,
61:        emailNormalized: "",
62:        roles: ["OWNER"],
63:        status: "ACTIVE",
64:      });
65:    }
66:
67:    if (profile.status !== "ACTIVE") {
68:      throw new AuthenticationError("User is not active.", { userId: profile.userId });
69:    }
70:
71:    // Step 5: reject tokens issued before global logout watermark.
72:    if (profile.globalLogoutAfter && claims.issuedAt < profile.globalLogoutAfter) {
73:      throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (global logout).", { userId: profile.userId });
74:    }
75:
76:    let deviceSession;
77:    if (claims.deviceId) {
78:      deviceSession = await this.users.getDeviceSession(mapping.tenantId, mapping.userId, claims.deviceId);
79:      if (deviceSession) {
80:        if (deviceSession.status === "REVOKED") {
81:          throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (device logout).", { deviceId: claims.deviceId });
82:        }
83:        if (deviceSession.deviceLogoutAfter && claims.issuedAt < deviceSession.deviceLogoutAfter) {
84:          throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (device logout).", { deviceId: claims.deviceId });
85:        }
86:      }
87:    }
88:
89:    const sessionId = deviceSession?.sessionId ?? this.ids.newSessionId();
90:
91:    return {
92:      requestId: input.requestId,
93:      correlationId: input.correlationId,
94:      principal: {
95:        userId: profile.userId,
96:        cognitoSubject: claims.sub,
97:        sessionId,
98:        deviceId: claims.deviceId,
99:      },
100:      tenant: {
101:        tenantId: profile.tenantId,
102:        roles: profile.roles,
103:      },
104:      auth: {
105:        issuedAt: claims.issuedAt,
106:        expiresAt: claims.expiresAt,
107:        tokenId: claims.tokenId,
108:      },
109:    };
110:  }
111:}

11:## Histórico do debate
12:- **Rodada 1** — propostas independentes. Convergência forte em estrutura e na maioria das 17 ameaças em comum. A proposta do Codex é adotada como base — mais rigorosa: tagueamento STRIDE explícito por ameaça, calibração de severidade consistentemente mais alta e melhor justificada (6 ameaças "Alta" vs. nenhuma na proposta do Claude, incluindo achados reais que o Claude não tinha visto: session theft/CSP não definida, PDF processado sem sandbox de parser dedicado, supply-chain sem pin por digest/SBOM, dependency compromise sem política de atualização formal). A proposta do Claude cobre 2 pontos que a do Codex não menciona: SSRF associado especificamente a FUT-002 (webhooks de saída futuros) e o vazamento de números/e-mails tratado como decisão consciente de não criptografar em nível de aplicação (vs. apenas listado como ameaça a mitigar).

19:|---|---|---|---|
20:| **Account takeover — S/E** | Credential stuffing, reset de conta, comprometimento do e-mail. | Cognito; rate limit e proteção contra credential stuffing; MFA ao menos opcional (`requirements.md` SEC-001); revogação de sessões no runbook (`disaster-recovery.md` §7). | MFA obrigatório permanece indefinido (UNK-006); recuperação de conta não foi detalhada. **Média**. |
21:| **Password abuse — S/D** | Brute force, senhas reutilizadas ou fracas, enumeração de usuários. | Cognito e rate limiting de login; WAF antes de produção pública (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §14). | Política de senha, respostas anti-enumeração e proteção adaptativa não especificadas. **Média**. |
22:| **Session theft — S/E** | Roubo de JWT/refresh token via XSS, dispositivo comprometido ou armazenamento inseguro. | JWT validado pelo API Gateway; autorização fina no domínio; procedimento de revogação (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §4; `disaster-recovery.md` §7). | Armazenamento do token, duração, rotação, **CSP** e revogação por dispositivo ausentes. **Alta**. |
23:| **Document malware — T/D** | Upload de executável, arquivo infectado ou formato disfarçado. | Magic bytes, GuardDuty Malware Protection, dois buckets, estados fail-closed, promoção apenas de `CLEAN` (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §7). | Falsos negativos e formatos não cobertos; fallback ainda condicional. **Média**. |
24:| **Malicious PDF — T/D/E** | Parser exploit, PDF poliglota, zip/decompression bomb, consumo extremo de CPU/memória. | Quarentena, tamanho máximo e GuardDuty; somente arquivos limpos seguem para OCR (Red Team). | Antimalware **não substitui sandbox de parser**; limites de páginas, complexidade e recursos não definidos. **Alta**. |
25:| **Prompt injection — T/E** | Texto no documento tenta comandar o LLM ou adulterar campos extraídos. | Conteúdo tratado como dado; schema validation, comparação de extratores, `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` fail-closed (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §10; `data-model.md` §2). | Testes adversariais e isolamento explícito do prompt continuam pendentes; alteração automática já é bloqueada, reduzindo impacto. **Média**. |
26:| **SSRF — I/D/E** | URL/referência embutida induz backend a acessar rede interna/metadados; hoje não há superfície, mas **FUT-002 (webhooks de saída) a introduzirá diretamente** se implementado sem controle. | Pipeline trabalha sobre objeto S3 conhecido; nenhuma funcionalidade de fetch arbitrário hoje. | Egress allowlist e bloqueio de redirects/URLs internas não definidos — **registrar como pré-requisito de design de FUT-002, não deixar para descobrir na implementação**. **Baixa hoje / condicionalmente relevante**. |
27:| **Injection — T/E** | NoSQL expression, command, template, header ou log injection. | DynamoDB elimina SQL; schemas versionados, WAF, validação de payload de IA. | Codificação contextual, construção segura de expressions e fuzzing não especificados. **Média**. |
28:| **Privilege escalation — E** | Manipular IDs, claims ou papéis para executar operação fora do escopo. | Autorização fina no domínio, IAM por função, testes negativos (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §§4,14). | Matriz de autorização por operação ainda não existe; RBAC futuro amplia a superfície. **Média**. |
29:| **Tenant escape — I/E/T** | IDOR, chave sem tenant, evento/worker processando tenant errado. | `tenantId` obrigatório em chaves/GSIs/S3/mensagens/eventos/idempotência; testes negativos inclusive em restore (`architecture-fase3-consolidada.md` §6; `disaster-recovery.md` §6). | Um erro de implementação no middleware/repositório ainda pode atravessar tenants; enforcement estrutural em código não definido. **Média**. |
30:| **Webhook spoofing — S/T/R** | Callback forjado, replay ou colisão de event ID. | Assinatura em tempo constante, timestamp/nonce, chave composta, inbox idempotente/reconciliável (Red Team; `data-model.md` §§2,4). | Rotação de segredos e peculiaridades por provedor dependem da implementação. **Média**. |
31:| **Leaked WhatsApp numbers / e-mails — I** | Número/e-mail exposto em payload, DynamoDB, logs, DLQ ou suporte. | KMS/criptografia at-rest do serviço gerenciado, logs sem PII, isolamento por tenant, retenção/purge, adapter por canal. **Decisão consciente**: sem criptografia adicional em nível de aplicação para esses campos — confia na criptografia at-rest do DynamoDB, não é lacuna, é trade-off registrado. | Redação específica de payloads/DLQs/consoles de suporte e mascaramento em interfaces administrativas não definidos. **Média**. |
32:| **Leaked documents — I** | Acesso indevido pelo app, credencial AWS, backup, URL ou bucket. | Dois buckets privados, SSE-KMS, IAM separado, tenant no object key, versionamento, testes negativos de restore. | Autorização de download e acesso operacional (suporte/admin) ainda não detalhados. **Alta**. |
33:| **Compromised provider — S/I/T** | SES/BSP/Textract/Bedrock ou credencial do fornecedor comprometida. | Adapters substituíveis, filas isoladas, Secrets Manager, kill switch, retenção local do trabalho, rotação (`disaster-recovery.md` §§5,7). | Provedor pode exfiltrar conteúdo já recebido; critérios de minimização/detecção/failover não definidos. **Alta**. |
34:| **Exposed presigned URLs — I/T** | URL vazada permite upload/download por terceiro durante sua validade. | Expiração em minutos, objeto/operação únicos, slot atômico, `content-length-range` (Red Team; `data-model.md` §4). | URL continua sendo bearer credential; TTL exato, download policy e resposta a vazamento não definidos. **Média**. |
35:| **S3 misconfiguration — I/T** | Bucket público, policy/KMS/OAC incorretos, promoção indevida. | Block Public Access, OAC, SSE-KMS, papéis separados, CDK, scans IaC, restore fail-closed. | Drift/configuração manual precisa ser detectado operacionalmente; desenho é forte. **Baixa**. |
36:| **Logging sensitive content — I/R** | PII/tokens/documentos/payloads completos em logs/traces. | Logs JSON sem PII, auditoria redigida, `tenantId` nunca dimensão de métrica. | Lista de campos proibidos, redactor central e testes automatizados não definidos. **Média**. |
37:| **Secrets leakage — I/E** | Segredo em Git, variável, log, artefato ou role excessiva. | Secrets Manager, GitHub Actions via OIDC, IAM least privilege, scans, runbook de rotação. | TTL/rotação por segredo, secret scanning pré-commit, contenção automatizada ausentes. **Média**. |
38:| **Supply-chain attack — T/E** | Workflow, action, registry, build runner ou artefato adulterado. | SAST/dependency/IaC scans, aprovação manual, deploy canário, tag imutável. | **Sem pin por digest, proveniência assinada, SBOM ou verificação de artefato.** **Alta**. |
39:| **Dependency compromise — T/E/D** | Pacote npm malicioso, typosquatting, atualização comprometida. | Dependency scan e pipeline de testes. | Lockfile não basta; política de atualização, allowlist, análise de scripts, SLA de CVE não definidas. **Alta**. |
40:| **Cost abuse — D** | Flood de API/uploads, reprocessamento OCR/IA, webhook storm, disparos WhatsApp. | `TenantQuota`, slot de upload, reserved concurrency, quotas por canal, Budgets 80/100%, anomaly detection, kill switches (gate G6). | Ataque distribuído entre contas e custo antes do alarme disparar permanecem; WAF só entra na exposição pública. **Média**. |
41:
42:## Resumo residual
43:| Severidade | Quantidade | Ameaças |
44:|---|---:|---|
45:| **Alta** | 6 | session theft, malicious PDF, leaked documents, compromised provider, supply-chain, dependency compromise |
46:| **Média** | 13 | takeover, password abuse, malware, prompt injection, injection, privilege escalation, tenant escape, webhook spoofing, números/e-mails, presigned URLs, logs, secrets, cost abuse |
47:| **Baixa** | 2 | SSRF, S3 misconfiguration |
48:
49:## Lacunas novas (a fechar antes/durante a implementação)
50:1. **Arquitetura de sessão**: armazenamento no browser, TTL, refresh rotation, logout global/por dispositivo, **CSP**.
51:2. **Sandbox de parsing de PDF**: sem rede, com limites de páginas/memória/CPU/expansão — antimalware genérico não substitui isso.
52:3. **Matriz explícita de autorização por ação/recurso** + um único componente que deriva `tenantId` da identidade validada, nunca do request.
53:4. **Política de egress**: nenhum fetch arbitrário, bloqueio de metadata/private ranges, allowlist — pré-requisito de design antes de FUT-002 (webhooks de saída) ser implementado.
54:5. **Redactor central de logs** com schema de campos sensíveis, testado contra logs/traces/DLQs/eventos.
55:6. **Supply-chain hardening**: actions/imagens fixadas por digest, SBOM, assinatura/proveniência de artefato, verificação no deploy.
56:7. **Gestão formal de dependências**: lockfile obrigatório, scripts de instalação restritos, allowlist de pacotes críticos, SLA de resposta a CVE.
57:
58:## Pontos abertos (Claude, Rodada 1 — aguardando reação do Codex)
59:Nenhum ponto de discordância — a proposta do Codex foi adotada integralmente como base, com os 2 achados do Claude (SSRF condicionado a FUT-002, decisão consciente sobre criptografia de contato) incorporados sem conflito. Enviado ao Codex apenas para confirmação e nota final, não para nova rodada de divergência.

 succeeded in 1370ms:
1:/**
2: * Central redactor - implementation-blueprint.md #14.1, threat-model.md redactor gap.
3: * Loads the denylist from schemas/sensitive-fields.json (single source of truth shared
4: * with any future documentation/tooling) and scrubs field names + known value patterns
5: * before anything reaches logs, trace annotations, DLQ diagnostic metadata or operational
6: * events.
7: */
8:import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
9:import defaultSensitiveFieldsConfig from "../../../schemas/sensitive-fields.json";
10:
11:interface SensitiveFieldsConfig {
12:  redactedFieldNames: string[];
13:  redactedValuePatterns: { name: string; pattern: string }[];
14:  limits: {
15:    maxDepth: number;
16:    maxStringLength: number;
17:    maxArrayLength: number;
18:    maxObjectKeys: number;
19:  };
20:  redactedValuePlaceholder: string;
21:}
22:
23:const PLACEHOLDER_FALLBACK = "[REDACTED]";
24:const TRUNCATED_SUFFIX = "…[TRUNCATED]";
25:
26:// Statically imported (not read via fs+import.meta.url at runtime): esbuild inlines JSON
27:// imports as a JS object literal into the Lambda bundle at build time
28:// (infra/lib/scoped-lambda-function.ts's bundleEntry), which fixes two real bugs found in
29:// the M3.5 architecture review round 1: (1) `import.meta.url` is empty under the esbuild
30:// "cjs" output format, so `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` used to throw at module load in
31:// every real Lambda's cold start; (2) `schemas/` was never copied into the deployed bundle
32:// (`lambda.Code.fromAsset(outDir)` only contains the bundled index.js), so even a fixed
33:// path would have failed with ENOENT in AWS. Static import removes the runtime file
34:// dependency entirely - the config travels inside the bundle like any other imported module.
35:function loadConfig(explicitPath?: string): SensitiveFieldsConfig {
36:  if (explicitPath) {
37:    const raw = readFileSync(explicitPath, "utf-8");
38:    return JSON.parse(raw) as SensitiveFieldsConfig;
39:  }
40:  return defaultSensitiveFieldsConfig as SensitiveFieldsConfig;
41:}
42:
43:export interface RedactorOptions {
44:  /** Override for tests; defaults to schemas/sensitive-fields.json relative to this file. */
45:  configPath?: string;
46:}
47:
48:export class Redactor {
49:  private readonly fieldNames: Set<string>;
50:  private readonly valuePatterns: { name: string; regex: RegExp }[];
51:  private readonly limits: SensitiveFieldsConfig["limits"];
52:  private readonly placeholder: string;
53:
54:  constructor(options: RedactorOptions = {}) {
55:    const config = loadConfig(options.configPath);
56:    this.fieldNames = new Set(config.redactedFieldNames.map((f) => f.toLowerCase()));
57:    this.valuePatterns = config.redactedValuePatterns.map((p) => ({
58:      name: p.name,
59:      regex: new RegExp(p.pattern, "gi"),
60:    }));
61:    this.limits = config.limits;
62:    this.placeholder = config.redactedValuePlaceholder ?? PLACEHOLDER_FALLBACK;
63:  }
64:
65:  /** Redacts an arbitrary structured value (object/array/primitive) recursively. */
66:  redact(value: unknown): unknown {
67:    return this.redactValue(value, 0, new WeakSet());
68:  }
69:
70:  /** Redacts known value patterns (emails, bearer tokens, ...) inside a free-text string,
71:   * e.g. sanitized SDK/provider exception messages (implementation-blueprint.md #14.1). */
72:  redactString(value: string): string {
73:    let result = this.truncateString(value);
74:    for (const { regex } of this.valuePatterns) {
75:      regex.lastIndex = 0;
76:      result = result.replace(regex, this.placeholder);
77:    }
78:    return result;
79:  }
80:
81:  /** Redacts an Error, producing a plain object safe to log (never logs raw `.stack` of
82:   * SDK errors, which frequently embed request bodies/headers). */
83:  redactError(err: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
84:    if (err instanceof Error) {
85:      return {
86:        name: err.name,
87:        message: this.redactString(err.message),
88:        // Stack traces can leak values interpolated into error messages (SDKs do this a lot);
89:        // keep only the first line (type + message) to preserve some debuggability.
90:        stackSummary: this.redactString(err.stack?.split("\n")[0] ?? err.message),
91:      };
92:    }
93:    return { message: this.redactString(String(err)) };
94:  }
95:
96:  private truncateString(value: string): string {
97:    if (value.length <= this.limits.maxStringLength) {
98:      return value;
99:    }
100:    return value.slice(0, this.limits.maxStringLength) + TRUNCATED_SUFFIX;
101:  }
102:
103:  private redactValue(value: unknown, depth: number, seen: WeakSet<object>): unknown {
104:    if (depth > this.limits.maxDepth) {
105:      return "[MAX_DEPTH_EXCEEDED]";
106:    }
107:    if (value === null || value === undefined) {
108:      return value;
109:    }
110:    if (typeof value === "string") {
111:      return this.redactString(value);
112:    }
113:    if (typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean") {
114:      return value;
115:    }
116:    if (value instanceof Error) {
117:      return this.redactError(value);
118:    }
119:    if (Array.isArray(value)) {
120:      if (seen.has(value)) return "[CIRCULAR]";
121:      seen.add(value);
122:      return value.slice(0, this.limits.maxArrayLength).map((v) => this.redactValue(v, depth + 1, seen));
123:    }
124:    if (typeof value === "object") {
125:      if (seen.has(value as object)) return "[CIRCULAR]";
126:      seen.add(value as object);
127:      const entries = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>).slice(0, this.limits.maxObjectKeys);
128:      const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};
129:      for (const [key, v] of entries) {
130:        if (this.fieldNames.has(key.toLowerCase())) {
131:          out[key] = this.placeholder;
132:        } else {
133:          out[key] = this.redactValue(v, depth + 1, seen);
134:        }
135:      }
136:      return out;
137:    }
138:    // functions, symbols, bigint etc - never log these verbatim
139:    return "[UNSUPPORTED_TYPE]";
140:  }
141:}
142:
143:/** Shared default instance - stateless config load, safe to reuse across a Lambda's lifecycle. */
144:export const defaultRedactor = new Redactor();

1:/**
2: * Real DynamoDB adapter for IdentityStore (M3.5). Thin translation to
3: * DynamoDBDocumentClient commands - no OCC/idempotency logic lives here, that's already
4: * built by shared/dynamodb/occ.ts and shared/idempotency/idempotency.ts; this class only
5: * executes the commands those builders produce (for `update`) and issues plain
6: * Get/Put for the rest.
7: */
8:import { GetCommand, PutCommand } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
9:import type { DynamoDBDocumentClient } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
10:import type { EntityKey, IdentityStore } from "../ports/identity-store.js";
11:import { isConditionalCheckFailed, mapDynamoError } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/sdk-errors.js";
12:
13:export class DynamoDbIdentityStore implements IdentityStore {
14:  constructor(
15:    private readonly client: DynamoDBDocumentClient,
16:    private readonly tableName: string,
17:  ) {}
18:
19:  async get<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(key: EntityKey): Promise<T | undefined> {
20:    try {
21:      const result = await this.client.send(
22:        new GetCommand({ TableName: this.tableName, Key: key, ConsistentRead: true }),
23:      );
24:      return result.Item as T | undefined;
25:    } catch (err) {
26:      throw mapDynamoError(err, "IdentityStore.get");
27:    }
28:  }
29:
30:  async putIfAbsent<T extends EntityKey>(item: T): Promise<boolean> {
31:    try {
32:      await this.client.send(
33:        new PutCommand({
34:          TableName: this.tableName,
35:          Item: item,
36:          ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)",
37:        }),
38:      );
39:      return true;
40:    } catch (err) {
41:      if (isConditionalCheckFailed(err)) return false;
42:      throw mapDynamoError(err, "IdentityStore.putIfAbsent");
43:    }
44:  }
45:
46:  /** Unconditional overwrite - matches the fake's semantics (non-OCC bookkeeping writes only). */
47:  async update<T extends EntityKey>(item: T): Promise<void> {
48:    try {
49:      await this.client.send(new PutCommand({ TableName: this.tableName, Item: item }));
50:    } catch (err) {
51:      throw mapDynamoError(err, "IdentityStore.update");
52:    }
53:  }
54:}

1:/**
2: * User repository — data-model.md §2 (`TENANT#t#USER#u` / `PROFILE`) plus the session-

6: * this module keeps revocation watermarks on the User item itself (globalLogoutAfter) and

11:import type { EntityKey, IdentityStore } from "../ports/identity-store.js";
12:
13:export interface UserProfile {
14:  PK: string;
15:  SK: "PROFILE";
16:  entityType: "User";
17:  userId: string;
18:  tenantId: string;
19:  identitySubject: string;
20:  emailNormalized: string;
21:  roles: string[];
22:  status: "ACTIVE" | "SUSPENDED";
23:  globalLogoutAfter?: string;
24:  createdAt: string;
25:  updatedAt: string;
26:  version: number;
27:}
28:
29:export interface DeviceSession {
30:  PK: string;
31:  SK: string; // SESSION#<deviceId>
32:  entityType: "DeviceSession";
33:  tenantId: string;
34:  userId: string;
35:  deviceId: string;
36:  sessionId: string;
37:  refreshFamilyId: string;
38:  deviceLogoutAfter?: string;
39:  createdAt: string;
40:  lastSeenAt: string;
41:  expiresAt: string;
42:  status: "ACTIVE" | "REVOKED";
43:}
44:
45:export function userProfileKey(tenantId: string, userId: string): EntityKey {
46:  return { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#USER#${userId}`, SK: "PROFILE" };
47:}
48:
49:export function deviceSessionKey(tenantId: string, userId: string, deviceId: string): EntityKey {
50:  return { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#USER#${userId}`, SK: `SESSION#${deviceId}` };
51:}
52:
53:export class UserRepository {
54:  constructor(
55:    private readonly store: IdentityStore,
56:    private readonly now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
57:  ) {}
58:
59:  async getProfile(tenantId: string, userId: string): Promise<UserProfile | undefined> {
60:    return this.store.get<UserProfile>(userProfileKey(tenantId, userId));
61:  }
62:
63:  async createProfileIfAbsent(profile: Omit<UserProfile, "PK" | "SK" | "entityType" | "createdAt" | "updatedAt" | "version">): Promise<UserProfile> {
64:    const key = userProfileKey(profile.tenantId, profile.userId);
65:    const existing = await this.getProfile(profile.tenantId, profile.userId);
66:    if (existing) {
67:      return existing;
68:    }
69:    const now = this.now();
70:    const item: UserProfile = {
71:      ...key,
72:      SK: "PROFILE",
73:      entityType: "User",
74:      ...profile,
75:      createdAt: now,
76:      updatedAt: now,
77:      version: 1,
78:    };
79:    await this.store.putIfAbsent(item);
80:    return item;
81:  }
82:
83:  async getDeviceSession(tenantId: string, userId: string, deviceId: string): Promise<DeviceSession | undefined> {
84:    return this.store.get<DeviceSession>(deviceSessionKey(tenantId, userId, deviceId));
85:  }
86:
87:  async upsertDeviceSession(session: DeviceSession): Promise<void> {
88:    await this.store.update(session);
89:  }
90:
91:  /** Logout global — implementation-blueprint.md §4.2: revokes every token issued before now. */
92:  async logoutAll(tenantId: string, userId: string): Promise<void> {
93:    const profile = await this.getProfile(tenantId, userId);
94:    if (!profile) return;
95:    await this.store.update({ ...profile, globalLogoutAfter: this.now(), updatedAt: this.now() });
96:  }
97:
98:  /** Logout by device — revokes only this device's refresh family. */
99:  async logoutDevice(tenantId: string, userId: string, deviceId: string): Promise<void> {
100:    const session = await this.getDeviceSession(tenantId, userId, deviceId);
101:    if (!session) return;
102:    await this.store.update({ ...session, status: "REVOKED", deviceLogoutAfter: this.now() });
103:  }
104:}

1:/**

33:const defaultSink = (level: LogLevel, line: string): void => {
34:  // eslint-disable-next-line no-console -- this IS the sink; central logger is the sanctioned exception.
35:  const fn = level === "error" ? console.error : level === "warn" ? console.warn : console.log;
36:  fn(line);
37:};
38:
39:export class SecureLogger {
40:  private readonly redactor: Redactor;
41:  private readonly baseContext: LogContext;
42:  private readonly sink: (level: LogLevel, line: string) => void;
43:  private readonly now: () => string;
44:
45:  constructor(options: SecureLoggerOptions = {}) {
46:    this.redactor = options.redactor ?? defaultRedactor;
47:    this.baseContext = options.baseContext ?? {};
48:    this.sink = options.sink ?? defaultSink;
49:    this.now = options.now ?? (() => new Date().toISOString());
50:  }
51:
52:  /** Returns a new logger with additional persistent context (e.g. bind correlationId once per invocation). */
53:  child(context: LogContext): SecureLogger {
54:    return new SecureLogger({
55:      redactor: this.redactor,
56:      baseContext: { ...this.baseContext, ...context },
57:      sink: this.sink,
58:      now: this.now,
59:    });
60:  }
61:
62:  debug(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
63:    this.write("debug", event, context);
64:  }
65:
66:  info(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
67:    this.write("info", event, context);
68:  }
69:
70:  warn(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
71:    this.write("warn", event, context);
72:  }
73:
74:  error(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
75:    this.write("error", event, context);
76:  }
77:
78:  private write(level: LogLevel, event: string, context: LogContext): void {
79:    const merged = { ...this.baseContext, ...context };
80:    const redacted = this.redactor.redact(merged) as Record<string, unknown>;
81:    const line = JSON.stringify({
82:      timestamp: this.now(),
83:      level,
84:      event,
85:      ...redacted,
86:    });
87:    this.sink(level, line);
88:  }
89:}

27:/**
28: * M3.5: real bundling for handler entrypoints, replacing `lambda.Code.fromInline`
29: * placeholders (docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md, "Handlers Lambda reais" /
30: * "Bundling"). Invokes esbuild directly (target node20, one bundle per function, sourcemap
31: * external, no minify - stack traces stay auditable, per the design doc) rather than
32: * aws-cdk-lib's NodejsFunction, so ScopedLambdaFunction remains the single Lambda
33: * construction point (no second construct with its own bundling path to keep in sync).

36:function bundleEntry(entry: string, handlerExport: string): lambda.Code {
37:  const outDir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "expiration-tracker-lambda-"));
38:  const outFile = path.join(outDir, "index.js");
39:  // CJS output (not ESM, despite the project being "type": "module") so the bundle needs
40:  // no package.json/extension gymnastics inside the Lambda zip - just index.js + index.handler.
41:  esbuild.buildSync({
42:    entryPoints: [entry],
43:    outfile: outFile,
44:    bundle: true,
45:    platform: "node",
46:    target: "node20",
47:    format: "cjs",
48:    sourcemap: "external",
49:    minify: false,
50:  });
51:  if (handlerExport !== "handler") {
52:    throw new Error(`bundleEntry: only "handler" export name is wired into index.handler; got "${handlerExport}"`);
53:  }
54:  return lambda.Code.fromAsset(outDir);
55:}

159:export interface ScopedLambdaFunctionProps extends Omit<lambda.FunctionProps, "code" | "handler"> {
160:  access: AccessCapability[];
161:  /** Real bundling path (M3.5): TS/JS entrypoint, bundled via esbuild (see bundleEntry above)
162:   * into a CJS asset. Mutually exclusive with `code` (tests use `code` for inline synthetic
163:   * bodies; production handlers use `entry`). */
164:  entry?: string;
165:  /** Exported handler function name inside `entry`. Only "handler" is currently supported
166:   * (bundleEntry throws otherwise) - kept as an explicit prop rather than hardcoded so a
167:   * synth-time error names the mismatch instead of silently invoking the wrong export. */
168:  handlerExport?: string;
169:  /** Test-only escape hatch for inline/synthetic Lambda code. Mutually exclusive with `entry`. */
170:  code?: lambda.Code;
171:}
172:
173:/**
174: * Every Lambda in this system MUST be constructed via ScopedLambdaFunction, never
175: * `new lambda.Function` directly (implementation-blueprint.md §17.1) - it is the single
176: * enforcement point for least-privilege IAM per function.
177: */
178:export class ScopedLambdaFunction extends Construct {
179:  readonly function: lambda.Function;
180:
181:  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: ScopedLambdaFunctionProps) {
182:    super(scope, id);
183:
184:    for (const capability of props.access) {
185:      if (!KNOWN_CAPABILITY_KINDS.has(capability.kind)) {
186:        // "falha synth quando o recurso solicitado não corresponde a uma capability conhecida"
187:        throw new Error(
188:          `ScopedLambdaFunction(${id}): unknown access capability kind "${capability.kind}". ` +
189:            `Known kinds: ${[...KNOWN_CAPABILITY_KINDS].join(", ")}.`,
190:        );
191:      }
192:    }
193:
194:    const { access, code, entry, handlerExport, ...rest } = props;
195:
196:    if (entry && code) {
197:      throw new Error(`ScopedLambdaFunction(${id}): "entry" and "code" are mutually exclusive.`);
198:    }
199:
200:    const resolvedCode = entry
201:      ? bundleEntry(entry, handlerExport ?? "handler")
202:      : (code ?? lambda.Code.fromInline("exports.handler = async () => ({ statusCode: 501 });"));
203:
204:    this.function = new lambda.Function(this, "Function", {
205:      timeout: Duration.seconds(10),
206:      memorySize: 256,
207:      tracing: lambda.Tracing.ACTIVE,
208:      ...rest,
209:      handler: "index.handler",
210:      code: resolvedCode,
211:    });
212:
213:    for (const capability of access) {
214:      capability.grant(this.function);
215:    }
216:  }
217:}

34:export class SchemaRegistry {
35:  private readonly ajv: Ajv2020;
36:  private readonly compiled = new Map<string, ValidateFunction>();
37:
38:  /** Takes an explicit list of already-loaded schema objects - no filesystem access. Callers
39:   * that need "every schema under schemas/" (CLI/tests) use
40:   * `schema-registry-disk.ts#loadAllSchemasFromDisk()` instead of constructing this directly
41:   * with an empty/missing list. */
42:  constructor(schemas: object[]) {
43:    this.ajv = new Ajv2020({ strict: true, allErrors: true });
44:    addFormats(this.ajv);
45:    // Two passes not needed here since all schemas are added before any compile() call below
46:    // (compile is lazy, triggered by the first validate() per schemaId) - $ref between
47:    // schemas resolves correctly regardless of list order.
48:    for (const schema of schemas) {
49:      const withId = schema as { $id?: string };
50:      if (withId.$id) {
51:        this.ajv.addSchema(schema, withId.$id);
52:      }
53:    }
54:  }
55:
56:  /** Validates `data` against the schema identified by its `$id`. Returns { valid, errors }. */
57:  validate(schemaId: string, data: unknown): { valid: boolean; errors: string[] } {
58:    let validateFn = this.compiled.get(schemaId);
59:    if (!validateFn) {
60:      const fn = this.ajv.getSchema(schemaId);
61:      if (!fn) {
62:        throw new Error(`Unknown schema $id: ${schemaId}`);
63:      }
64:      validateFn = fn;
65:      this.compiled.set(schemaId, validateFn);
66:    }
67:    const valid = validateFn(data) as boolean;
68:    const errors = (validateFn.errors ?? []).map(
69:      (e) => `${e.instancePath || "/"} ${e.message ?? "invalid"}`,
70:    );
71:    return { valid, errors };
72:  }
73:}

76: * Production runtime singleton - imported by Lambda handlers (e.g. reminder-dispatch-
77: * handler.ts). Built from statically-imported schema modules so esbuild inlines them as JS
78: * object literals into the bundle at build time, same fix as Redactor's
79: * schemas/sensitive-fields.json (Arquitetura axis round1, commit 494f4e5). Do NOT change
80: * this back to `new SchemaRegistry()` (dynamic directory walk) - that depends on
81: * `import.meta.url`, which is empty under the esbuild "cjs" bundle format
82: * (infra/lib/scoped-lambda-function.ts's bundleEntry) and would silently resolve zero
83: * schemas at real Lambda cold start. New schema added under schemas/ that a handler needs
84: * at runtime: add both the file AND a static import line above.
85: */

1:/**
2: * Central redactor - implementation-blueprint.md #14.1, threat-model.md redactor gap.
3: * Loads the denylist from schemas/sensitive-fields.json (single source of truth shared
4: * with any future documentation/tooling) and scrubs field names + known value patterns
5: * before anything reaches logs, trace annotations, DLQ diagnostic metadata or operational
6: * events.
7: */
8:import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
9:import defaultSensitiveFieldsConfig from "../../../schemas/sensitive-fields.json";
10:
11:interface SensitiveFieldsConfig {
12:  redactedFieldNames: string[];
13:  redactedValuePatterns: { name: string; pattern: string }[];
14:  limits: {
15:    maxDepth: number;
16:    maxStringLength: number;
17:    maxArrayLength: number;
18:    maxObjectKeys: number;
19:  };
20:  redactedValuePlaceholder: string;
21:}
22:
23:const PLACEHOLDER_FALLBACK = "[REDACTED]";
24:const TRUNCATED_SUFFIX = "…[TRUNCATED]";
25:
26:// Statically imported (not read via fs+import.meta.url at runtime): esbuild inlines JSON
27:// imports as a JS object literal into the Lambda bundle at build time
28:// (infra/lib/scoped-lambda-function.ts's bundleEntry), which fixes two real bugs found in
29:// the M3.5 architecture review round 1: (1) `import.meta.url` is empty under the esbuild
30:// "cjs" output format, so `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` used to throw at module load in
31:// every real Lambda's cold start; (2) `schemas/` was never copied into the deployed bundle
32:// (`lambda.Code.fromAsset(outDir)` only contains the bundled index.js), so even a fixed
33:// path would have failed with ENOENT in AWS. Static import removes the runtime file
34:// dependency entirely - the config travels inside the bundle like any other imported module.
35:function loadConfig(explicitPath?: string): SensitiveFieldsConfig {
36:  if (explicitPath) {
37:    const raw = readFileSync(explicitPath, "utf-8");
38:    return JSON.parse(raw) as SensitiveFieldsConfig;
39:  }
40:  return defaultSensitiveFieldsConfig as SensitiveFieldsConfig;
41:}
42:
43:export interface RedactorOptions {
44:  /** Override for tests; defaults to schemas/sensitive-fields.json relative to this file. */
45:  configPath?: string;
46:}
47:
48:export class Redactor {
49:  private readonly fieldNames: Set<string>;
50:  private readonly valuePatterns: { name: string; regex: RegExp }[];
51:  private readonly limits: SensitiveFieldsConfig["limits"];
52:  private readonly placeholder: string;
53:
54:  constructor(options: RedactorOptions = {}) {
55:    const config = loadConfig(options.configPath);
56:    this.fieldNames = new Set(config.redactedFieldNames.map((f) => f.toLowerCase()));
57:    this.valuePatterns = config.redactedValuePatterns.map((p) => ({
58:      name: p.name,
59:      regex: new RegExp(p.pattern, "gi"),
60:    }));
61:    this.limits = config.limits;
62:    this.placeholder = config.redactedValuePlaceholder ?? PLACEHOLDER_FALLBACK;
63:  }
64:
65:  /** Redacts an arbitrary structured value (object/array/primitive) recursively. */
66:  redact(value: unknown): unknown {
67:    return this.redactValue(value, 0, new WeakSet());
68:  }
69:
70:  /** Redacts known value patterns (emails, bearer tokens, ...) inside a free-text string,
71:   * e.g. sanitized SDK/provider exception messages (implementation-blueprint.md #14.1). */
72:  redactString(value: string): string {
73:    let result = this.truncateString(value);
74:    for (const { regex } of this.valuePatterns) {
75:      regex.lastIndex = 0;
76:      result = result.replace(regex, this.placeholder);
77:    }
78:    return result;
79:  }
80:
81:  /** Redacts an Error, producing a plain object safe to log (never logs raw `.stack` of
82:   * SDK errors, which frequently embed request bodies/headers). */
83:  redactError(err: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
84:    if (err instanceof Error) {
85:      return {
86:        name: err.name,
87:        message: this.redactString(err.message),
88:        // Stack traces can leak values interpolated into error messages (SDKs do this a lot);
89:        // keep only the first line (type + message) to preserve some debuggability.
90:        stackSummary: this.redactString(err.stack?.split("\n")[0] ?? err.message),
91:      };
92:    }
93:    return { message: this.redactString(String(err)) };
94:  }
95:
96:  private truncateString(value: string): string {
97:    if (value.length <= this.limits.maxStringLength) {
98:      return value;
99:    }
100:    return value.slice(0, this.limits.maxStringLength) + TRUNCATED_SUFFIX;
101:  }
102:
103:  private redactValue(value: unknown, depth: number, seen: WeakSet<object>): unknown {
104:    if (depth > this.limits.maxDepth) {
105:      return "[MAX_DEPTH_EXCEEDED]";
106:    }
107:    if (value === null || value === undefined) {
108:      return value;
109:    }
110:    if (typeof value === "string") {
111:      return this.redactString(value);
112:    }
113:    if (typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean") {
114:      return value;
115:    }
116:    if (value instanceof Error) {
117:      return this.redactError(value);
118:    }
119:    if (Array.isArray(value)) {
120:      if (seen.has(value)) return "[CIRCULAR]";
121:      seen.add(value);
122:      return value.slice(0, this.limits.maxArrayLength).map((v) => this.redactValue(v, depth + 1, seen));
123:    }
124:    if (typeof value === "object") {
125:      if (seen.has(value as object)) return "[CIRCULAR]";
126:      seen.add(value as object);
127:      const entries = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>).slice(0, this.limits.maxObjectKeys);
128:      const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};
129:      for (const [key, v] of entries) {
130:        if (this.fieldNames.has(key.toLowerCase())) {
131:          out[key] = this.placeholder;
132:        } else {
133:          out[key] = this.redactValue(v, depth + 1, seen);
134:        }
135:      }
136:      return out;
137:    }
138:    // functions, symbols, bigint etc - never log these verbatim
139:    return "[UNSUPPORTED_TYPE]";
140:  }
141:}

1:/**
2: * SecureLogger - implementation-blueprint.md #14.1: "Todos os handlers usam SecureLogger.
3: * Chamadas diretas a console.* falham no lint" (enforced by .eslintrc.cjs no-console rule,

9: * shared/observability/metrics.ts exists yet - this constraint is aspirational until that
10: * module is built (tracked as a real gap under Observability & Operability, not this logger's
11: * job to enforce). Do not treat this comment as evidence metrics.ts exists.

13:import { defaultRedactor, Redactor } from "./redactor.js";
14:
15:export type LogLevel = "debug" | "info" | "warn" | "error";
16:
17:export interface LogContext {
18:  correlationId?: string;
19:  tenantId?: string;
20:  requestId?: string;
21:  [key: string]: unknown;
22:}
23:
24:export interface SecureLoggerOptions {
25:  redactor?: Redactor;
26:  /** Base context merged into every log line (e.g. service name, function name). */
27:  baseContext?: LogContext;
28:  /** Injectable sink for tests; defaults to console. */
29:  sink?: (level: LogLevel, line: string) => void;
30:  now?: () => string;
31:}
32:
33:const defaultSink = (level: LogLevel, line: string): void => {
34:  // eslint-disable-next-line no-console -- this IS the sink; central logger is the sanctioned exception.
35:  const fn = level === "error" ? console.error : level === "warn" ? console.warn : console.log;
36:  fn(line);
37:};
38:
39:export class SecureLogger {
40:  private readonly redactor: Redactor;
41:  private readonly baseContext: LogContext;
42:  private readonly sink: (level: LogLevel, line: string) => void;
43:  private readonly now: () => string;
44:
45:  constructor(options: SecureLoggerOptions = {}) {
46:    this.redactor = options.redactor ?? defaultRedactor;
47:    this.baseContext = options.baseContext ?? {};
48:    this.sink = options.sink ?? defaultSink;
49:    this.now = options.now ?? (() => new Date().toISOString());
50:  }
51:
52:  /** Returns a new logger with additional persistent context (e.g. bind correlationId once per invocation). */
53:  child(context: LogContext): SecureLogger {
54:    return new SecureLogger({
55:      redactor: this.redactor,
56:      baseContext: { ...this.baseContext, ...context },
57:      sink: this.sink,
58:      now: this.now,
59:    });
60:  }
61:
62:  debug(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
63:    this.write("debug", event, context);
64:  }
65:
66:  info(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
67:    this.write("info", event, context);
68:  }
69:
70:  warn(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
71:    this.write("warn", event, context);
72:  }
73:
74:  error(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
75:    this.write("error", event, context);
76:  }
77:
78:  private write(level: LogLevel, event: string, context: LogContext): void {
79:    const merged = { ...this.baseContext, ...context };
80:    const redacted = this.redactor.redact(merged) as Record<string, unknown>;
81:    const line = JSON.stringify({
82:      timestamp: this.now(),
83:      level,
84:      event,
85:      ...redacted,
86:    });
87:    this.sink(level, line);
88:  }
89:}
90:
91:export const logger = new SecureLogger();

27:/**
28: * M3.5: real bundling for handler entrypoints, replacing `lambda.Code.fromInline`
29: * placeholders (docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md, "Handlers Lambda reais" /
30: * "Bundling"). Invokes esbuild directly (target node20, one bundle per function, sourcemap
31: * external, no minify - stack traces stay auditable, per the design doc) rather than
32: * aws-cdk-lib's NodejsFunction, so ScopedLambdaFunction remains the single Lambda
33: * construction point (no second construct with its own bundling path to keep in sync).
34: * AWS SDK v3 is bundled IN (not left to the Lambda runtime-provided version), per design.
35: */
36:function bundleEntry(entry: string, handlerExport: string): lambda.Code {
37:  const outDir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "expiration-tracker-lambda-"));
38:  const outFile = path.join(outDir, "index.js");
39:  // CJS output (not ESM, despite the project being "type": "module") so the bundle needs
40:  // no package.json/extension gymnastics inside the Lambda zip - just index.js + index.handler.
41:  esbuild.buildSync({
42:    entryPoints: [entry],
43:    outfile: outFile,
44:    bundle: true,
45:    platform: "node",
46:    target: "node20",
47:    format: "cjs",
48:    sourcemap: "external",
49:    minify: false,
50:  });
51:  if (handlerExport !== "handler") {
52:    throw new Error(`bundleEntry: only "handler" export name is wired into index.handler; got "${handlerExport}"`);
53:  }
54:  return lambda.Code.fromAsset(outDir);
55:}

57:export interface AccessCapability {
58:  readonly kind: string;
59:  readonly description: string;
60:  grant(fn: lambda.Function): void;
